  "prompt": "Security audit of Phase 63 (read-chain-navigation-and-rotation-core) — verify threat mitigations exist in the implemented code. Produce SECURITY.md.\n\nIMPORTANT OUTPUT LOCATION: Write the security report to `.planning/phases/63-read-chain-navigation-and-rotation-core/63-SECURITY.md` — do NOT write or append to the repo-root `SECURITY.md`. If you find yourself about to touch repo-root SECURITY.md, stop and write to the phase path instead.\n\n<files_to_read>\n- .planning/phases/63-read-chain-navigation-and-rotation-core/63-0*-PLAN.md (threat models / must_haves)\n- .planning/phases/63-read-chain-navigation-and-rotation-core/63-CONTEXT.md (locked decisions, esp. zeroization D-09 carryover, D-05, D-08 anti-malicious-relay)\n- .planning/phases/63-read-chain-navigation-and-rotation-core/63-VERIFICATION.md (what passed)\n- docs/adr/0002-read-revocation-protects-future-content-only.md and docs/adr/0003-aad-bound-node-seal-encoding.md (the threat-model stance + frozen AAD encoding)\n- The implemented source: packages/sdk-core/src/{share/navigate.ts,share/grant.ts,rotation/engine.ts,rotation/scope.ts,folder/metadata-ops.ts,folder/registration.ts,folder/load.ts}; packages/sdk/src/{share/index.ts,client.ts}\n- CLAUDE.md (Critical Security Rules section)\n</files_to_read>\n\n<audit_focus>\nThis is a client-side zero-knowledge crypto phase. Verify in the actual committed code:\n1. **Zeroization — terminal-owner-only (the #1 risk this phase)**: rotation MINTS readKey'/fileKey' and may zero those on its own failure paths, but must NEVER zero caller-supplied parent keys or reused session keys. The grant/invite re-wrap path zeroes intermediate ephemeral material in a finally block but not caller buffers. Check engine.ts (rotateOne mint+zero), grant.ts (claimInviteReadKey reWrapKey finally), navigate.ts. A wrong zero here broke 48/89 sdk-e2e historically — flag any callee zeroing a reused/caller-owned buffer.\n2. **AAD-bound seal integrity (ADR-0003)**: navigation/rotation must CALL the frozen codec (sealNode/unsealNode/sealChildReadKey/unsealChildReadKey) and never reimplement AES/AAD. The navigation generation-source rule: AAD uses the PARENT's SealedChildRef.generation (mirror), never the child's own envelope generation — confirm navigate.ts does this (a wrong source = silent decrypt failure or staleness bypass).\n3. **No key leakage**: no privateKey/readKey logged, stored in localStorage/sessionStorage, or sent unencrypted to the server. Grants ECIES-wrap before persistence (issueReadGrant). The grant-issuance callback seam (insertShareFn) must only ever transmit the ECIES-wrapped readDescriptorRef, never the raw readKey.\n4. **Anti-malicious-relay (D-08 / §3.9)**: hasCoveringGrant cross-checks the client's own local grant record independently of the relay-supplied active-grant set — the relay set is a completeness aid, never an authority. Confirm scope.ts treats the relay set as untrusted.\n5. **Read-revocation honesty (ADR-0002)**: rotation protects FUTURE content/navigation/filenames only, not already-distributed content or prior versions — confirm nothing in the code overclaims (e.g. no false \"this revokes past access\" assumption).\n6. **Scope-exit gating**: a private (no covering grant) mutation must NOT rotate (no needless key exposure / publish) — verified by the zero-rotation invariant test; confirm the gating logic is sound.\n\nClassify findings by severity (block on high). Note that the 4 Phase-64 soundness seams (mintFileKeyOnRotate etc.) are INTENTIONALLY deferred — do NOT flag their absence as a vulnerability; the read-revocation soundness guarantees (content rekey, grant re-mint) are explicitly Phase 64 per ADR-0002 + CONTEXT D-01. Phase 63 is the navigation/rotation skeleton.\n</audit_focus>\n\nReturn: overall verdict (SECURED / findings), count by severity, and the path of the written 63-SECURITY.md."
}

Rebuild the client chain so dist matches source (REQUIRED — cross-package dist staleness):\n   ```\n   cd $(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)\n   pnpm --filter @cipherbox/crypto build && pnpm --filter @cipherbox/core build \\\n     && pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api-client build && pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk-core build \\\n     && pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk build && pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api build\n   ```\n   If any build fails, STOP and report the build error (that itself is a gate failure).\n\n2. Restart the API on :3000 (kill the stale one first):\n   ```\n   lsof -nP -iTCP:3000 -sTCP:LISTEN -t | xargs -r kill -9\n   ( cd apps/api && PORT=3000 node dist/main.js > /tmp/ship-phase-63-api.log 2>&1 & )\n   ```\n   Health-poll until ready: `for i in $(seq 1 30); do curl -fsS http://localhost:3000/health >/dev/null 2>&1 && break; sleep 1; done` then confirm `curl -fsS http://localhost:3000/health`. Returns a typed `'ok' | 'behind-retry' | 'revoked'` union (no ambiguous null). ---
---
created: 2026-02-14
title: Add ERC-1271 contract wallet authentication support
area: auth
files:
  - apps/api/src/auth/services/siwe.service.ts
  - apps/web/src/components/auth/WalletLoginButton.tsx
---

## Problem

Phase 12.3 SIWE implementation only verifies EOA signatures via `ecrecover` (viem's `verifyMessage`). Smart contract wallets (Safe, Argent, Sequence, etc.) use ERC-1271 `isValidSignature` for signature verification, which requires an on-chain call to the wallet contract on mainnet.

Without ERC-1271 support, users with smart contract wallets will get "Invalid SIWE signature" errors when attempting to log in or link their wallet.

## Solution

TBD — key considerations:

- Backend needs an RPC connection to Ethereum mainnet to call `isValidSignature(hash, signature)` on the wallet contract
- viem's `verifyMessage` with a `publicClient` already supports ERC-1271 fallback — pass a client connected to mainnet
- Need to decide: free RPC (rate-limited) vs paid provider (Alchemy/Infura) vs user-provided RPC
- Could detect contract wallets by checking `eth_getCode` on the address first, and only use RPC path for non-empty code
- Consider cost/latency implications of on-chain calls during auth flow
```

```
=== design §3.5 move-within-scope ===
### 3.5 Move within scope — `O(1)`, 0 ECIES, 2 republishes, no rotation

Remove the `SealedChildRef` from the old parent (reseal + republish); add it to the new parent (reseal + republish). The node keeps its own `readKey`/`generation`. Kills the move-reencrypt storm.

Caveat from review **m2 (per-grant scope):** "within scope" is a **per-grant** property, not a global one. A reader granted at the **old parent only** cached the moved node's `readKey`; after a move to a sibling they do not cover, "within scope for the owner" is "out of scope for that reader." Therefore: **any move that changes a node's ancestor set must rotate if any active grant sits on an ancestor that is no longer an ancestor.** Because FUSE gains a grant-root concept (Section 3.9) and already holds the mounted tree, it can compute **exact per-grant scope** rather than the conservative "rotate on any ancestor-set change" — a move that is genuinely within-scope for all grants (owner-only, or both parents under the same grant root) stays at 2 republishes. This disposes of old open question Q3 (no over-rotation on benign within-scope moves).

### 3.6 Delete / move-out / rename-over — rotate **iff** scope exit (not "always rotate")
```

> AGENT

The design §3.5 clarifies it: move-within-scope = "Remove the `SealedChildRef` from the old parent (**reseal** + republish); add it to the new parent (**reseal** + republish)." So a re-seal of the child readKey under the **new** parent's readKey is part of the move. FLAG-63-U2 says the implemented `moveItem` doesn't re-seal — a genuine gap vs §3.5, where a grantee navigating via the destination path would fail AAD auth.

This is squarely Phase-64 territory (move/rotation/scope-exit interplay) and both the verifier and security auditor deferred it as non-blocking. Per the ship rule (risky/cross-domain → defer + capture + note in PR), I'll **not** block the ship but capture it as a high-visibility todo and surface it to you. Writing both deferred todos.

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.planning/todos/pending/2026-06-29-move-within-scope-reseal-child-readkey.md",
  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-29\ntitle: move-within-scope must reseal the child readKey under the new parent (design §3.5)\narea: sdk-core\nresolves_phase: 64\nfiles:\n  - packages/sdk-core/src/folder/metadata-ops.ts\n  - packages/sdk-core/src/__tests__/folder.test.ts\n---\n\n## Problem\n\nSurfaced by the Phase-63 security audit as **FLAG-63-U2** (non-blocking, deferred to Phase 64/68).\n\nDesign §3.5 (\"Move within scope\") specifies: *\"Remove the `SealedChildRef` from the old parent (**reseal** + republish); add it to the new parent (**reseal** + republish). The node keeps its own `readKey`/`generation`.\"* The \"reseal\" is required because a `SealedChildRef.readKeySealed` seals the child's `readKey` under the **parent's** `readKey` (via `sealChildReadKey`). The two parents have different `readKey`s.\n\nThe Phase-63 `moveItem` (`packages/sdk-core/src/folder/metadata-ops.ts`) is a **pure link rewrite that does NOT call `sealChildReadKey`** (confirmed by the Phase-63 verifier: \"moveItem … sealChildReadKey and sealNode not called\", and `folder.test.ts` asserts this). It relocates the existing `SealedChildRef` — still sealed under the **source** parent's `readKey` — into the destination parent's child list verbatim.\n\nConsequence: a grantee navigating to the moved node **via the destination path** calls `unsealChildReadKey(readKeySealed, destParentReadKey, …)`, but the ref was sealed under `srcParentReadKey` → AES-GCM AAD authentication fails → navigation breaks for the moved node under the new parent.\n\nPhase 63 matched its (oversimplified) plan/ROADMAP wording \"link rewrites only (zero re-encryption)\", where \"re-encryption\" was read as content/body re-encryption only. The app is intentionally non-runnable mid-milestone, so this is not yet exercised end-to-end.\n\n## Solution\n\nIn Phase 64 (or wherever the move flow is composed end-to-end), make move-within-scope **re-seal the child `readKey` under the destination parent's `readKey`** before adding the ref to the new parent:\n\n- Compute `newReadKeySealed = sealChildReadKey(childReadKey, destParentReadKey, aad(child.id, child.kind, child.generation))` and store it on the relocated `SealedChildRef`. The child keeps its own `readKey`/`generation` (no content re-encryption, no `fileKey` change) — so \"zero re-encryption\" of content still holds; only the parent-link seal is rewritten.\n- This requires the caller to supply both the source and destination parent `readKey`s (the unwrap of the child readkey at source, the reseal at dest). Decide whether `moveItem` takes these directly or whether the re-seal lives in the higher-level move flow that already holds the parent keys.\n- Note the §3.5 \"per-grant scope\" subtlety (review m2): a move that changes a node's ancestor set must additionally **rotate iff** an active grant sits on an ancestor that is no longer an ancestor (this is the scope-exit predicate `hasCoveringGrant`, already present in `rotation/scope.ts`). The reseal-under-new-parent and the scope-exit-rotation are distinct concerns — both must be wired.\n- Add a unit test: move a node to a new parent, then navigate from the destination and assert `'ok'` with the correct recovered content key (currently no test covers dest-path navigation after a move).\n\n## References\n\n- `.planning/design/2026-06-26-sharing-read-keychaining-design.md` §3.5 (move within scope), §3.6 (scope-exit rotation)\n- `.planning/phases/63-read-chain-navigation-and-rotation-core/63-SECURITY.md` FLAG-63-U2\n- ROADMAP Phase 64 (Rotation Soundness — Revocation Guarantees)\n"
}

This is **safe in Phase 63**: `unsealNode` without a write key produces a node with no `writeBody`, and `sealNode` skips the write-body reseal when there is no `writeBody` (confirmed at `packages/core/src/node/seal.ts` ~L118). Phase 63 is read-chain only.\n\nLatent risk: if a Phase-65+ code path invokes `rotateOne` on a node that **does** carry a `writeBody` before the real write-chain key is wired, the write body would be resealed under an all-zeros key. The Phase-65 seam comment in `engine.ts` (~L324-328) acknowledges this.\n\n## Solution\n\nWhen Phase 65 adds write-chain rotation:\n\n- Thread the node's real `writeKey` (or a freshly minted `writeKey'`) into `rotateOne` instead of the all-zeros placeholder, and reseal the write body under it.\n- Remove the `PLACEHOLDER_WRITE_KEY` and its seam comment once the real key is wired.\n- Add a test covering rotation of a node that has a `writeBody`.\n\n## References\n\n- `.planning/phases/63-read-chain-navigation-and-rotation-core/63-SECURITY.md` FLAG-63-U1\n- `docs/adr/0001-write-revocation-full-ed25519-rotation.md` (write-body context, Phase 65)\n- ROADMAP Phase 65\n"

The Phase-63 happy path (single root-step rotation; first-level child hits the Phase-64 stub) is verified working and does NOT reach these paths.\n\nFindings (CodeRabbit severities in brackets):\n\n- **[CRITICAL] BFS drops the rotated child link** (`engine.ts` ~L335-341, ~L495-554): `rotateOne` computes `newReadKeySealed` via `sealChildReadKey`, but the BFS caller drops the returned value and never writes it back to the parent's `SealedChildRef.readKeySealed`/`.generation`. A multi-level subtree rotation therefore would not propagate the new child key to parents. Fix the BFS loop to persist `newReadKeySealed` (and the generation mirror) onto the parent ref and republish. (Related test gap: [MAJOR] `engine.test.ts` ~L247-268 only asserts `sealChildReadKey` was *called*, not that its output updates the parent ref and is republished — strengthen it.)\n- **[CRITICAL] Placeholder write-key publish fallback** (`engine.ts` ~L346-349): the publish path falls back to `PLACEHOLDER_WRITE_KEY` when `ipnsPrivateKey` is undefined. Never publish an IPNS record signed/sealed with a placeholder — require a real key or fail closed. (The write-body placeholder itself is tracked separately in `2026-06-29-rotateone-placeholder-writekey-phase65.md` for Phase 65; this finding is about the *publish* path guarding against undefined inputs.)\n- **[MAJOR] Job-record completion marked too early** (`engine.ts` ~L376-383): `jobRecord.completedNodeIds.add(nodeId)` happens before downstream steps (e.g. `reMintGrantsRootedAt`); on retry/resume a failed re-mint is skipped because the node is already \"complete\". Move the add to after the node is fully processed.\n- **[MAJOR] Resume fast-path marks complete incorrectly** (`engine.ts` ~L288-290): when `rootNodeId` is already in `completedNodeIds`, `rotateReadFromNode` can mark the resumed walk complete and skip processing `jobRecord.frontier`, bypassing the `verifySubtreeClean` resume seam. Fix the resume guard.\n- **[MAJOR] Terminal job status not persisted** (`engine.ts` ~L557-558): the normal completion path updates `jobRecord.status` only in memory; persist the terminal state via the host-injected persistence callback on successful finish (ties into ROT-07 durable resume, Phase 68, but the in-engine ordering is Phase 64).\n- **[MAJOR] BFS queue leaks derived child read keys** (`engine.ts` ~L495-554): child read keys stored in queue entries are not zeroed after use; zero them once a queue item's children are derived/enqueued (terminal-owner — these are engine-derived, safe to zero).\n- **[MAJOR] Missing resume + failure-path tests** (`engine.test.ts` ~L292-312, ~L315-352): add a resumed-rotation test (`completedNodeIds` non-empty) and a failure-path zeroization test (force publish/seal rejection after `readKeyPrime` is minted; assert the minted key is zeroed and caller-supplied `parentReadKey` is NOT).\n\n## Solution\n\nAddress as part of Phase 64's rotation-soundness implementation, when the four seams are filled and the resumable walk + `verifySubtreeClean` convergence are built. Re-run the full CodeRabbit review on the Phase-64 branch to confirm closure.\n\n## References\n\n- `.planning/design/2026-06-26-sharing-read-keychaining-design.md` §4.2 (ordering), §4.5 (rotateOne 9-step / per-node commit / crash recovery / verifySubtreeClean), §4.4 (HIGH-3 re-mint)\n- `.planning/phases/63-read-chain-navigation-and-rotation-core/63-CONTEXT.md` D-01 (the 63→64 seam line), D-10 (job record)\n- ROADMAP Phase 64 (Rotation Soundness — Revocation Guarantees); requirements ROT-03..ROT-06\n"
}

If the destination publish fails, the item is lost from both folders. Reorder so the destination publish is confirmed before the source removal is committed (or make the pair atomic / recoverable). Ties into the [[move-within-scope-reseal-child-readkey]] re-seal work and the known `folderTree`/sequence-number reconcile discipline (web #489/#494).\n- **[MAJOR] Descendant pushed to result before readability confirmed** (`client.ts` ~L1958-1981): the child/descendant traversal pushes entries to `result` before `sdkCore.loadFolderMetadata` confirms the descendant is actually readable, so an unreadable descendant can appear in enumeration. Move the `result.push` so it only happens after a successful load.\n\n## Solution\n\nAddress alongside Phase 64's move/rotation hardening (and Phase 68 `folderTree` reconcile for the durable web path). Add tests covering: a failed destination publish must not lose the item from the source; an unreadable descendant must not appear in traversal results.\n\n## References\n\n- `.planning/phases/63-read-chain-navigation-and-rotation-core/63-SECURITY.md` (move re-seal context, FLAG-63-U2)\n- [[move-within-scope-reseal-child-readkey]]\n- ROADMAP Phase 64; Phase 68 (web rotation UX + folderTree reconcile)\n"

=== comment #3 registration.ts:180 (CRITICAL) full body ===
_🗄️ Data Integrity & Integration_ | _🔴 Critical_ | _🏗️ Heavy lift_

**Require stable `nodeId` and `nodeGeneration` on metadata updates.**

Line 174 mints a fresh UUID and Line 175 resets generation when callers omit these fields. That breaks existing parent `SealedChildRef` bindings because new child refs are sealed with `childId`/`childGeneration` in `addFilePointerToFolder`; after an update, navigation will validate against the newly published node identity instead of the identity used when the child key was sealed. Make these required for updates, or load/preserve them from the current node before sealing.

-  nodeGeneration?: number;
+  nodeGeneration: number;
@@
-        id: params.nodeId ?? crypto.randomUUID(),
-        generation: params.nodeGeneration ?? 0,
+        id: params.nodeId,
+        generation: params.nodeGeneration,
```
</details>

=== #7 grant.ts:106 (validate rootGeneration) ===
_🎯 Functional Correctness_ | _🟡 Minor_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Validate `rootGeneration` before persisting the grant.**

`rootGeneration` becomes the stale-read witness consumed by `navigateReadChain`. Right now `-1` makes every later read immediately return `behind-retry`, and `NaN` makes the `>` check stop working entirely. Reject non-integer / negative values up front.


<summary>Suggested fix</summary>

```diff

   shareRootReadKey: Uint8Array;
   recipientPublicKey: Uint8Array;
   rootNodeId: string;
   rootIpnsName: string;
   rootGeneration: number;

 }): Promise<{ shareId: string; readDescriptorRef: string }> {
   // Input validation — reject malformed keys before any crypto work or persistence.
   if (params.shareRootReadKey.length !== 32) {
     throw new Error(
       `issueReadGrant: shareRootReadKey must be 32 bytes, got ${params.shareRootReadKey.length}`
     );

{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.planning/todos/pending/2026-06-29-update-folder-metadata-preserve-node-identity.md",
  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-29\ntitle: updateFolderMetadataAndPublish mints a fresh UUID / resets generation on every update — preserve node identity\narea: sdk-core\nresolves_phase: 64\nfiles:\n  - packages/sdk-core/src/folder/registration.ts\n  - packages/sdk/src/client.ts\n---\n\n## Problem\n\nFlagged CRITICAL by the Phase-63 PR CodeRabbit review (`registration.ts:180`).\n\n`updateFolderMetadataAndPublish` (`packages/sdk-core/src/folder/registration.ts` ~L174-175) builds the folder Node with:\n\n```\nid: params.nodeId ?? crypto.randomUUID(),\ngeneration: params.nodeGeneration ?? 0,\n```\n\nAll **six** call sites in `packages/sdk/src/client.ts` (L493, L558, L581, L629, L747, L1006) omit `nodeId` and `nodeGeneration` (confirmed by grep — neither field is passed anywhere in client.ts). Therefore **every folder metadata update mints a brand-new random UUID and resets `generation` to 0**.\n\nTwo invariants are broken on update:\n\n1. **Node identity instability** — the folder Node's `id` is part of the AAD the parent used when it sealed the folder's child read-key (`sealChildReadKey` with `child.id`/`kind`/`generation` in `addFilePointerToFolder`). Changing the folder's `id` on a metadata update means a grantee navigating from the parent validates against a different identity than the one the key was sealed under → AAD authentication fails → navigation to the updated folder breaks.\n2. **Generation reset** — `generation` is the rotation counter (one of the three counters that must never be conflated: `generation` / `keyEpoch` / `sequenceNumber`). A metadata update (add/move/delete/rename) bumps the IPNS `sequenceNumber` via CAS; it must NOT reset `generation` to 0. Resetting it corrupts the staleness-witness the read path (`navigateReadChain`) compares against and the rotation engine relies on.\n\nNot caught by Phase-63 tests/e2e because the happy-path (create → navigate → root-rotate) does not perform a folder metadata UPDATE that is then navigated by a grantee against the pre-update binding. The app is intentionally non-runnable mid-milestone.\n\nThis is the same parent-child binding-stability class as [[move-within-scope-reseal-child-readkey]].\n\n## Solution\n\nMake node identity stable across metadata updates (Phase 64, alongside the move re-seal + rotation hardening):\n\n- Make `nodeId` and `nodeGeneration` **required** on `updateFolderMetadataAndPublish` (CodeRabbit's suggested type change: `nodeId: string`, `nodeGeneration: number`; drop the `?? crypto.randomUUID()` / `?? 0` fallbacks), OR load the current node inside the function and preserve its `id`/`generation` before sealing.\n- Thread the stable `id` + current `generation` through all six `client.ts` call sites from the loaded folder state (the client holds the folder's IPNS name + state; it must also carry the node UUID + current generation).\n- Add a unit test asserting that an update preserves the folder Node's `id` and does not reset `generation`, and an e2e/integration test that updates a shared folder's metadata and confirms a grantee can still navigate to it from the parent.\n\n## References\n\n- `.planning/phases/63-read-chain-navigation-and-rotation-core/63-CONTEXT.md` (generation single-source-of-truth; the three counters)\n- `docs/METADATA_SCHEMAS.md` (node/v3 schema + generation invariant)\n- CodeRabbit PR #579 review, `registration.ts:180`\n- [[move-within-scope-reseal-child-readkey]]; ROADMAP Phase 64\n"
}

  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-29\ntitle: createSubfolder accepts teeKeys but does not wire encryptedIpnsPrivateKey/keyEpoch — fail closed or wire TEE republish\narea: sdk-core\nresolves_phase: 67\nfiles:\n  - packages/sdk-core/src/folder/registration.ts\n---\n\n## Problem\n\nFlagged Major (Security & Privacy) by the Phase-63 PR CodeRabbit review (`registration.ts:42-101`, an outside-diff body comment).\n\n`createSubfolder` accepts `teeKeys` (the TEE-encrypted IPNS private key + key epoch used so the TEE worker can republish the subfolder's IPNS record), but the publish path does not set `encryptedIpnsPrivateKey`/`keyEpoch`, and the function returns them unset. A new subfolder created this way would therefore not be picked up by the TEE republisher and its IPNS record would eventually expire.\n\nPhase 63 is the read-chain/rotation skeleton and intentionally does not wire TEE republishing (TEE lease-renewer contract is Phase 67; the app is non-runnable mid-milestone). The risk is silent: a caller that supplies `teeKeys` expecting republish wiring gets a subfolder that is not republish-enrolled, with no error.\n\n## Solution\n\nEither:\n- **Fail closed now (cheap guard):** if `teeKeys` are supplied to `createSubfolder` but the publish path does not yet wire them, throw a clear \"TEE republish wiring not implemented (Phase 67)\" error rather than silently publishing without them — so no caller mistakenly believes a subfolder is republish-enrolled. (Low-risk; can be done before Phase 67 if any caller starts passing teeKeys.)\n- **Wire it (Phase 67):** thread `encryptedIpnsPrivateKey` + `keyEpoch` into the published record and the return value as part of the TEE lease-renewer integration.\n\nVerify whether any current caller passes `teeKeys` (grep) — if none do, this is latent param surface and the fail-closed guard is sufficient interim hardening.\n\n## References\n\n- CodeRabbit PR #579 review, `registration.ts:42-101`\n- CLAUDE.md TEE Republishing architecture; ROADMAP Phase 67 (TEE lease-renewer contract)\n"
}
```

## Phase 63: Read-Chain Navigation and Rotation Core — done end-to-end

**Plan → Execute → Ship**, all autonomous while you were AFK. PR: **https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/579**

### Outcome
- **Planned**: 7 plans / 4 waves; plan-checker PASSED (iter 2); 7/7 requirements + 10/10 decisions covered.
- **Executed**: all 7 plans (sequential, no worktrees), ~30 commits. Delivered navigation walk + typed `NavigateResult`, `issueReadGrant`/`claimInviteReadKey`, rotation `engine.ts` (named file, +4 Phase-64 seams), folder mutations, `hasCoveringGrant` + the SC#4 zero-rotation invariant, and `reWrapForRecipients` deletion.
- **Verified**: 5/5 success criteria. **Secured**: 27/27 threats. **Nyquist**: compliant. sdk-core 261 / sdk 169 unit tests pass; live sdk-e2e round-trip passes.
- **PR review**: CodeRabbit (20 findings) + greptile (3) triaged — **all 13 threads resolved**. 7 in-scope fixes applied (2 commits), the rest deferred with todos.
- **CI**: fully green (Build, Test, Typecheck, SDK E2E, Lint, Release Preview, codecov, cross-language parity, lint-pr-title; Cargo skipped — TS-only).

### ⚠️ Two CRITICAL correctness gaps deferred to Phase 64 — please review
Both are real and matched their plan spec (so verification passed), but flagged by CodeRabbit/greptile/security audit. They live in code paths the verified happy-path doesn't reach yet, and Phase 64 ("Rotation Soundness") owns them. Captured as todos:
1. **`moveItem` doesn't re-seal the child readKey under the new parent** (design §3.5) → a grantee navigating via the destination path would fail AAD auth. `move-within-scope-reseal-child-readkey`
2. **`updateFolderMetadataAndPublish` mints a fresh UUID + resets generation on every update** (all 6 callers omit `nodeId`/`nodeGeneration`) → breaks parent binding + the generation witness. `update-folder-metadata-preserve-node-identity`

Other deferrals (todos under `.planning/todos/pending/`): rotation-engine walk/resume soundness (Phase 64), placeholder writeKey (Phase 65), client move/publish durability (64/68), TEE republish wiring (67), base64 dedup nit (64).

### Notes
- **Commit signing** was disabled for the session as you asked — no commits hung; the PR commits are unsigned (you may want to confirm branch protection accepts that, or re-sign before merge).
- A **weekly usage limit** briefly hit mid-execution (~07:00 Berlin) during 63-06; it reset immediately and I resumed cleanly — the tree was untouched at the interruption.
- The **merge decision is yours.** I did not merge.

One judgment call worth surfacing: I deferred the two CRITICAL items rather than fixing them mid-ship, because both are heavy-lift, squarely in Phase 64's domain, and fixing them blind could have broken the passing tests. If you'd rather I pull either fix forward into this PR before merge, say the word.

```
=== full body of greptile engine.ts:97 thread ===
 **`newReadKeySealed` is sealed under the wrong key for Phase 64**

`rotateOne` receives the node's **own** pre-rotation readKey as `parentReadKey` (confirmed by the DESIGN NOTE in `rotateReadFromNode` and the BFS queue entries that store `nodeReadKey: childReadKey`). As a result, step 7 computes:

`sealChildReadKey(readKeyPrime, nodeOwnOldReadKey, nodeId, kind, genPrime)`

But for the parent's `SealedChildRef[N].readKeySealed` to work — the value the doc-comment here promises — the sealing key must be the **parent's new readKey** (`parentNewReadKeyPrime`), not the child's own old key. When Phase 64 implements the parent-link publish and uses `result.newReadKeySealed` to update the parent's children array, subsequent calls to `unsealChildReadKey` will pass the parent's new readKey and hit an AEAD authentication failure for every non-root rotated node.

The root of the confusion is the stale doc-comment on `RotateOneParams.parentReadKey` (line ~133) which still says "For all other nodes this is the NEW readKey' minted when the parent was rotated" — the pre-E2E-bug-fix wording. After the fix, `parentReadKey` is the node's own old readKey, and the two design goals (unsealing the node's read-body, computing the sealed child-ref for the parent) each require a different key. Phase 64 will need either a separate parameter or an out-of-band re-seal using the parent's `childReadKey` return value.
```

  "old_string": "- **[CRITICAL] BFS drops the rotated child link** (`engine.ts` ~L335-341, ~L495-554): `rotateOne` computes `newReadKeySealed` via `sealChildReadKey`, but the BFS caller drops the returned value and never writes it back to the parent's `SealedChildRef.readKeySealed`/`.generation`. A multi-level subtree rotation therefore would not propagate the new child key to parents. Fix the BFS loop to persist `newReadKeySealed` (and the generation mirror) onto the parent ref and republish. (Related test gap: [MAJOR] `engine.test.ts` ~L247-268 only asserts `sealChildReadKey` was *called*, not that its output updates the parent ref and is republished — strengthen it.)",
  "new_string": "- **[CRITICAL] BFS drops the rotated child link** (`engine.ts` ~L335-341, ~L495-554): `rotateOne` computes `newReadKeySealed` via `sealChildReadKey`, but the BFS caller drops the returned value and never writes it back to the parent's `SealedChildRef.readKeySealed`/`.generation`. A multi-level subtree rotation therefore would not propagate the new child key to parents. Fix the BFS loop to persist `newReadKeySealed` (and the generation mirror) onto the parent ref and republish. (Related test gap: [MAJOR] `engine.test.ts` ~L247-268 only asserts `sealChildReadKey` was *called*, not that its output updates the parent ref and is republished — strengthen it.)\n  - **[CRITICAL refinement — greptile P1, `engine.ts:97/337`]** `newReadKeySealed` is currently sealed under the **wrong key**. After the 63-07 e2e fix, `RotateOneParams.parentReadKey` carries the node's OWN pre-rotation readKey (used to unseal the node's read-body), so step-7 computes `sealChildReadKey(readKeyPrime, nodeOwnOldReadKey, …)`. But for the parent's `SealedChildRef[N].readKeySealed` to unseal, the seal key must be the **parent's NEW readKey'** (`parentNewReadKeyPrime`), not the child's own old key. So simply writing `newReadKeySealed` back to the parent (the bullet above) is NOT sufficient — Phase 64 must either pass the parent's new readKey as a separate param or re-seal out-of-band using the child's returned `childReadKey`, or every non-root rotated node will hit an AEAD auth failure on later `unsealChildReadKey`. (The misleading `parentReadKey` doc-comment was corrected in the Phase-63 ship; the field name remains a legacy misnomer pending this rework.)"
}
